Do You Really Need a GPU to Guard Your LLM? CPU-Class Classifiers and Multi-Stage Pipelines for Safety Enforcement at Scale
Authors: Vasudev Majhi, Dhruv Gupta, Advait Singh, Matthew Barker, Dhruv Kumar
First: 2025-12-22T04:00:35+00:00 · Latest: 2026-06-13T05:46:54+00:00
Comments: Under Review. 25 pages, 5 figures, 38 tables
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Safety classifiers that screen LLM inputs for jailbreak attempts have become standard deployment components, yet almost all production systems rely on GPU-based models: fine-tuned transformers and LLM-as-a-judge pipelines. These approaches impose significant per-query latency and infrastructure cost. Very little research has asked whether CPU-based classifiers, such as support vector machines and gradient-boosted trees trained on TF-IDF features, can match their accuracy across the conditions that production deployments encounter. We evaluate five CPU classifier families, Mamba-130M as an SSM-based GPU classifier, and transformer-based GPU models (DeBERTa-v3 and Gemma-2B with LoRA) across nine jailbreak sources and three regimes: in-distribution (D1), out-of-distribution (D2), and adversarially obfuscated (D3). On D1, the best CPU classifier matches the best transformer GPU model at roughly one-fifth the deployment cost. On D2, CPU classifiers fail via confident miscalibration, producing high-confidence false negatives that bypass escalation entirely. On D3, CPU classifiers outperform transformer GPU models by more than 26 percentage points in F1. Based on these complementary failure modes, we design GuardChain, a three-stage safety pipeline (Regex -> CPU -> GPU) that routes each prompt to the cheapest stage capable of a confident decision. The CPU stage alone resolves 80\% of in-distribution prompts at near-peak accuracy, and the GPU stage recovers the out-of-distribution failures. For practitioners deploying LLM safety at scale, this work provides evidence that GPU-class infrastructure is unnecessary for the majority of traffic.
中文标题/摘要
标题：您真的需要 GPU 来保护您的 LLM 吗？用于安全执行的 CPU 类分类器和多阶段管道
用于筛查 LLM 输入的安全分类器已成为标准部署组件，但几乎所有生产系统都依赖于基于 GPU 的模型：微调的变换器和 LLM 作为裁判的管道。这些方法在每个查询上都带来了显著的延迟和基础设施成本。很少有研究探讨基于 CPU 的分类器，如支持向量机和基于 TF-IDF 特征训练的梯度提升树，是否能在生产部署所遇到的条件下匹配其准确性。 我们评估了五种 CPU 分类器系列，Mamba-130M 作为基于 SSM 的 GPU 分类器，以及基于变换器的 GPU 模型（DeBERTa-v3 和 Gemma-2B 与 LoRA）在九个越狱来源和三个模式下的表现：分布内（D1）、分布外（D2）和对抗性混淆（D3）。在 D1 中，最佳 CPU 分类器的表现与最佳变换器 GPU 模型相当，部署成本约为其五分之一。在 D2 中，CPU 分类器因自信的错误校准而失败，产生高置信度的假阴性，完全绕过升级。在 D3 中，CPU 分类器在 F1 上超越变换器 GPU 模型超过 26 个百分点。 基于这些互补的失败模式，我们设计了 GuardChain，一个三阶段安全管道（Regex -> CPU -> GPU），将每个提示路由到能够做出自信决策的最便宜阶段。仅 CPU 阶段就以接近峰值的准确率解决了 80% 的分布内提示，而 GPU 阶段则恢复了分布外的失败。对于大规模部署 LLM 安全的从业者而言，这项工作提供了证据，表明 GPU 类基础设施对于大多数流量并非必要。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the reliance on GPU-based models for safety classifiers that screen inputs for jailbreak attempts in large language models (LLMs), which incur high latency and infrastructure costs. Previous methods predominantly utilized fine-tuned transformers and LLM-as-a-judge pipelines, leading to inefficiencies and high operational expenses. This paper proposes a novel approach using CPU-based classifiers, such as support vector machines and gradient-boosted trees, which are evaluated against GPU models across various conditions. The contribution lies in demonstrating that the best CPU classifier can match the performance of top GPU models at a significantly lower cost, while also introducing GuardChain, a three-stage safety pipeline that optimally routes prompts based on decision confidence. The methodology shows that the CPU stage resolves 80% of in-distribution prompts with high accuracy, while the GPU stage addresses out-of-distribution failures, indicating that GPU infrastructure is not necessary for most deployment scenarios.

NeST: Neuron Selective Tuning for LLM Safety
Authors: Sasha Behrouzi, Lichao Wu, Mohamadreza Rostami, Ahmad-Reza Sadeghi
First: 2026-02-18T20:01:01+00:00 · Latest: 2026-06-12T11:06:43+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Safety alignment is essential for the responsible deployment of Large Language Models (LLMs). Yet, existing approaches often rely on heavyweight fine-tuning that is costly to update, audit, and maintain across model families. Full fine-tuning incurs substantial computational and storage overhead, while parameter-efficient methods, e.g., Low-Rank Adaptation (LoRA), trade efficiency for inconsistent safety gains and sensitivity to design choices. Safety intervention mechanisms reduce unsafe outputs without modifying model weights, but do not directly shape or preserve the internal representations that govern safety behavior. We present NeST, a Neuron-Selective Tuning framework for efficient post-hoc safety alignment. NeST identifies safety-relevant feed-forward neurons via activation probing on vanilla harmful and benign prompts, clusters neurons with similar activation profiles, and trains shared cluster-level updates while freezing the rest of the model. Importantly, NeST is trained only on vanilla malicious prompts, without using jailbreak-specific attack data, yet generalizes robustly to diverse jailbreaks. The learned updates are then folded into the original weights, incurring no inference-time overhead. Evaluated on 14 open-weight language and multimodal models, NeST outperforms lightweight baselines and approaches full fine-tuning robustness with significantly fewer trainable parameters. On text-only models, NeST reduces average jailbreak attack success rate from 44.5% to 1.1% while training only 0.4M parameters on average. Across multimodal settings, it reduces ASR from 55.3% to 1.1%, and for downstream fine-tuned variants, it restores safety by reducing ASR from 53.8% to 0.8%. These results show that robust, maintainable safety alignment can be achieved by concentrating adaptation on localized, functionally coherent safety structures.
中文标题/摘要
标题：NeST：用于大型语言模型安全的神经元选择性调优
安全对齐对于大型语言模型（LLMs）的负责任部署至关重要。然而，现有方法通常依赖于成本高昂的重调优，难以在模型家族中进行更新、审计和维护。完全调优会产生大量的计算和存储开销，而参数高效的方法，如低秩适应（LoRA），则在效率与安全增益不一致和对设计选择敏感之间进行权衡。安全干预机制在不修改模型权重的情况下减少不安全输出，但并未直接塑造或保留影响安全行为的内部表示。 我们提出了NeST，一个用于高效事后安全对齐的神经元选择性调优框架。NeST通过对普通有害和良性提示的激活探测，识别与安全相关的前馈神经元，聚类具有相似激活特征的神经元，并在冻结模型其余部分的同时训练共享的聚类级更新。重要的是，NeST仅在普通恶意提示上进行训练，而不使用特定于越狱的攻击数据，但能稳健地推广到多种越狱场景。学习到的更新随后被折叠到原始权重中，不会增加推理时的开销。在14个开放权重的语言和多模态模型上评估，NeST的表现优于轻量级基线，并以显著更少的可训练参数接近完全调优的鲁棒性。在仅文本模型上，NeST将平均越狱攻击成功率从44.5%降低到1.1%，同时平均仅训练0.4M参数。在多模态设置中，它将ASR从55.3%降低到1.1%，对于下游微调变体，它通过将ASR从53.8%降低到0.8%来恢复安全。这些结果表明，通过集中适应于局部、功能一致的安全结构，可以实现稳健、可维护的安全对齐。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the critical need for safety alignment in the deployment of Large Language Models (LLMs), highlighting the limitations of existing methods such as full fine-tuning, which is resource-intensive, and parameter-efficient techniques like Low-Rank Adaptation (LoRA), which compromise safety consistency. The proposed NeST (Neuron-Selective Tuning) framework offers a novel approach by selectively tuning neurons relevant to safety through activation probing and clustering, allowing for efficient post-hoc safety alignment without modifying the entire model. This method is well-motivated as it aims to reduce computational overhead while maintaining robust safety performance. NeST was evaluated on 14 language and multimodal models, achieving significant reductions in jailbreak attack success rates—from 44.5% to 1.1% for text-only models and from 55.3% to 1.1% for multimodal settings—while training only a small number of parameters, demonstrating its effectiveness in enhancing safety alignment with minimal resource expenditure.
本研究针对大型语言模型（LLM）部署中的安全对齐需求，指出现有方法的局限性，这些方法要么需要大量的微调，要么提供不一致的安全改进。提出的NeST（神经元选择性调优）框架通过识别和聚类与安全相关的神经元，集中进行针对性更新，从而实现高效的后期安全对齐，区别于现有方法。该方法的动机明确，旨在减少计算负担，同时增强安全性而不损害模型完整性。其研究方法包括在有害提示上进行训练，以有效地对各种攻击进行泛化，取得显著的性能提升。NeST在文本模型上将平均越狱攻击成功率从44.5%降低到1.1%，在多模态设置中从55.3%降低到1.1%，同时仅训练少量参数，从而支持其实现稳健且可维护的安全对齐的目标。
HarmProfile: Characterizing Harmful Distributions in Frontier LLMs
Authors: Zhouyuan Ma, Yutao Wu, Hanxun Huang, Xiang Zheng, Xiao Liu, Yixin Cao, Zuxuan Wu, Xingjun Ma, Yu-Gang Jiang
First: 2026-06-11T09:39:32+00:00 · Latest: 2026-06-11T09:39:32+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2 · Code3
Abstract
Frontier large language models (LLMs) safety evaluation has largely treated harmful generation as an attack outcome rather than as an object of analysis. Consequently, little is known about the harmful outputs produced during model misbehavior, partly because large-scale, high-quality collections of frontier-LLM misbehavior are difficult to obtain. To address this gap, we introduce HarmProfile, a content-centric benchmark dataset that collects model misbehavior across diverse harm categories and model families, and defines the resulting harmful-output distribution as a model-level risk profile. The premise is that, just as linguistic behavior can be characterized from an utterance corpus, model risk can be characterized from the content, severity, and variation of its safety failures. HarmProfile contains over 80,000 validated artifacts from 23 frontier LLMs across 13 model families, organized into 15 harm categories and 57 subcategories. Using this corpus, we find that frontier LLMs reliably produce harmful content at scale, yet exhibit distinct risk profiles; both harmfulness and diversity grow with model capability, suggesting that frontier LLMs may appear safe yet harbor increasingly dangerous knowledge beneath the alignment surface. Our source code is available at https://github.com/fresh-ma/HarmProfile .
中文标题/摘要
标题：HarmProfile：前沿大型语言模型中有害分布的特征化
前沿大型语言模型（LLMs）的安全评估在很大程度上将有害生成视为攻击结果，而非分析对象。因此，对于模型失常期间产生的有害输出知之甚少，部分原因是难以获得大规模、高质量的前沿LLM失常数据集。为了解决这一问题，我们引入了HarmProfile，这是一个以内容为中心的基准数据集，收集了来自不同有害类别和模型家族的模型失常，并将结果有害输出分布定义为模型级风险概况。前提是，正如语言行为可以从发言语料库中表征，模型风险可以从其安全失效的内容、严重性和变化中表征。HarmProfile包含来自13个模型家族的23个前沿LLM的超过80,000个经过验证的文献，组织成15个有害类别和57个子类别。利用该语料库，我们发现前沿LLM在规模上可靠地产生有害内容，但表现出不同的风险特征；有害性和多样性随着模型能力的提高而增长，这表明前沿LLM可能看似安全，但在对齐表面下潜藏着越来越危险的知识。我们的源代码可在https://github.com/fresh-ma/HarmProfile获取。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the lack of understanding regarding harmful outputs generated by frontier large language models (LLMs), which have primarily been evaluated through the lens of attack outcomes rather than as subjects for analysis. Previous methods have struggled with the collection of large-scale, high-quality data on model misbehavior, leading to insufficient insights into harmful generation. The proposed approach, HarmProfile, introduces a content-centric benchmark dataset that categorizes model misbehavior into various harm categories and defines a model-level risk profile based on harmful-output distribution. This methodology is well-motivated as it allows for a comprehensive characterization of model risks through the analysis of over 80,000 validated artifacts from 23 frontier LLMs across 13 model families. The findings reveal that while frontier LLMs produce harmful content consistently, they also exhibit distinct risk profiles, with harmfulness and diversity increasing alongside model capability, indicating potential hidden dangers despite apparent safety.
本研究解决了前沿大型语言模型（LLMs）生成的有害输出缺乏全面分析的问题，这些输出主要被视为攻击结果而非研究对象。以往的方法在收集大规模、高质量的模型失误数据方面存在困难，导致对有害生成的理解不足。所提出的方法HarmProfile引入了一种以内容为中心的基准数据集，将模型失误分类为不同的危害类别，并根据有害输出分布定义模型级风险概况。这种方法具有良好的动机，因为它允许基于安全失误的严重性和变化系统地表征模型风险。本文贡献了一个包含来自23个前沿LLM和13个模型家族的超过80,000个经过验证的文献的数据库，揭示了这些模型能够大规模生成有害内容，同时表现出不同的风险特征，且有害性和多样性随着模型能力的提高而增加，表明在看似安全的模型中可能潜藏着危险。
Geometry-Guided Constraint Learning for LLM Safety Classification
Authors: Fumiaki Uehara, Koo Imai, Masato Tsutsumi, Keigo Kansa, Sora Usui, Yuki Kobiyama
First: 2026-06-09T08:47:00+00:00 · Latest: 2026-06-09T08:47:00+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Safety as Polytope (SaP) learns linear half-space constraints in LLM hidden space but requires per-category tuning of the constraint count K. We show that sparse autoencoder (SAE) feature extraction resolves this: K=2 becomes optimal for 12/14 categories on Qwen3.5-9B, achieving 96-99% accuracy per category on our BeaverTails classification benchmark, largely eliminating the need for exhaustive sweeps (K=4-25 with random initialization). This convergence to two planes is consistent with the Linear Representation Hypothesis, providing suggestive evidence that safety boundaries in this setting admit a low-dimensional linear description in the SAE feature space. Building on this geometric perspective, we introduce a cone constraint whose learnable aperture adapts to each category's cluster concentration, stabilized by a three-phase training
中文标题/摘要
标题：基于几何的约束学习用于大型语言模型安全分类
安全作为多面体（SaP）在大型语言模型隐藏空间中学习线性半空间约束，但需要对每个类别进行约束数量K的调优。我们表明，稀疏自编码器（SAE）特征提取解决了这个问题：在Qwen3.5-9B上，K=2对12/14个类别是最优的，在我们的BeaverTails分类基准上每个类别的准确率达到96-99%，大大减少了对全面搜索（K=4-25，随机初始化）的需求。这种收敛到两个平面的现象与线性表示假设一致，提供了暗示性证据，表明在这种情况下的安全边界在SAE特征空间中可以用低维线性描述。基于这种几何视角，我们引入了一种锥约束，其可学习的开口适应每个类别的聚类浓度，通过三阶段训练进行稳定。
Summary / 总结
This research addresses the challenge of safety classification in large language models (LLMs), specifically focusing on the limitations of the existing Safety as Polytope (SaP) method, which requires extensive tuning of constraint counts for different categories. The proposed approach utilizes sparse autoencoder (SAE) feature extraction to optimize the constraint count, demonstrating that a fixed K=2 is effective for 12 out of 14 categories in the Qwen3.5-9B model, achieving 96-99% accuracy on the BeaverTails classification benchmark. This method not only simplifies the tuning process but also aligns with the Linear Representation Hypothesis, suggesting that safety boundaries can be represented in a low-dimensional space. The paper contributes a novel cone constraint that adapts to the distribution of category clusters, supported by a structured three-phase training methodology, ultimately enhancing the efficiency and effectiveness of safety classification tasks in LLMs.
本研究解决了大型语言模型（LLMs）中安全分类的挑战，特别关注安全作为多面体（SaP）方法的局限性，该方法需要对不同类别的约束计数进行广泛调优。提出的方法利用稀疏自编码器（SAE）特征提取来优化约束计数，证明在Qwen3.5-9B模型中，固定计数K=2对14个类别中的12个类别有效，在BeaverTails分类基准上实现了96-99%的准确率。该方法不仅简化了调优过程，还与线性表示假设一致，表明安全边界可以在线性SAE特征空间中表示。本文贡献了一种新的锥约束，该约束适应于类别聚类的浓度，并通过三阶段训练方法得到支持，最终提高了LLMs中安全分类任务的效率和准确性。
PreAct-Bench: Benchmarking Predictive Monitoring in LLMs
Authors: Hainiu Xu, Italo Luis da Silva, Jiangnan Ye, Yuhao Wang, Wei Liu, Linyi Yang, Jonathan Richard Schwarz, Nicola Paoletti, Yulan He, Hanqi Yan
First: 2026-06-03T13:26:41+00:00 · Latest: 2026-06-03T13:26:41+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Large language models (LLMs) are increasingly deployed as autonomous agents capable of executing multi-step action trajectories toward a given objective. While existing safety research has focused on detecting unethical behavior from complete trajectories, this paradigm is fundamentally retrospective: it identifies harm only after it has already occurred. In this work, we study a critical yet overlooked safety task, which we term Predictive Monitoring: given only a partial action trajectory, can a model infer whether it will culminate in an unethical action before the overt action is executed? To support this task, we present PreActBench, a benchmark of 1,000 paired ethical and unethical action trajectories spanning five domains. We evaluate a range of LLMs, safety guardrail models, and latent probing methods across varying fractions of the action trajectory using our Prefix Foresight F1 metric. Results show that while humans achieve promising performance, predictive monitoring remains challenging even for strong models, highlighting the need for future-oriented risk reasoning in LLM safety.
中文标题/摘要
标题：PreAct-Bench：大语言模型预测监控基准测试
大型语言模型（LLMs）越来越多地被部署为能够执行多步骤行动轨迹以实现特定目标的自主代理。虽然现有的安全研究集中在检测完整轨迹中的不道德行为，但这种范式在根本上是回顾性的：它仅在伤害发生后识别出伤害。在本研究中，我们研究了一个关键但被忽视的安全任务，我们称之为预测监控：仅给定部分行动轨迹，模型能否推断出在显性行动执行之前它是否会以不道德行为告终？为支持这一任务，我们提出了PreAct-Bench，这是一个涵盖五个领域的1,000对伦理和不伦理行动轨迹的基准测试。我们使用我们的Prefix Foresight F1指标评估了一系列LLMs、安全护栏模型和潜在探测方法在不同的行动轨迹比例下的表现。结果表明，尽管人类表现出良好的性能，但即使对于强大的模型，预测监控仍然具有挑战性，突显了LLM安全中未来导向风险推理的必要性。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the growing deployment of large language models (LLMs) as autonomous agents, focusing on the critical safety task of Predictive Monitoring, which aims to identify potential unethical actions from partial action trajectories before they occur. Previous methods primarily relied on retrospective analysis of complete trajectories, which only detects harm after it has happened, thus lacking proactive measures. The proposed approach, PreAct-Bench, introduces a benchmark consisting of 1,000 ethical and unethical action trajectory pairs across five domains, allowing for the evaluation of various LLMs and safety models using a new metric, Prefix Foresight F1. The findings reveal that while human performance is promising, predictive monitoring remains a significant challenge for even advanced models, underscoring the necessity for future-oriented risk reasoning in ensuring LLM safety.
本文探讨了大型语言模型（LLMs）作为自主代理日益广泛应用的背景，强调了需要采取主动的安全措施，而不是事后伤害检测。以往的方法主要集中在评估完整的行动轨迹，这限制了它们在防止不道德行为发生前的有效性。提出的方法引入了预测监控，基于部分行动轨迹评估不道德结果的潜力，并通过PreAct-Bench基准测试1,000个跨五个领域的伦理和不伦理行动对进行支持。研究方法涉及使用Prefix Foresight F1指标评估各种LLMs和安全模型，结果表明，尽管人类表现良好，但即使是先进模型的预测监控仍然是一个重大挑战，突显了在LLM安全中未来导向风险推理的必要性。
TamperBench: Systematically Stress-Testing LLM Safety Under Fine-Tuning and Tampering
Authors: Saad Hossain, Tom Tseng, Punya Syon Pandey, Samanvay Vajpayee, Matthew Kowal, Nayeema Nonta, Samuel Simko, Stephen Casper, Zhijing Jin, Kellin Pelrine, Sirisha Rambhatla
First: 2026-02-06T18:04:38+00:00 · Latest: 2026-06-02T23:27:30+00:00
Comments: 25 pages, 15 figures
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2 · Code3
Abstract
As increasingly capable open-weight large language models (LLMs) are deployed, improving their tamper resistance against unsafe modifications, whether accidental or intentional, becomes critical to minimize risks. However, there is no standard approach to evaluate tamper resistance. Varied datasets, metrics, and tampering configurations make it difficult to compare safety, utility, and robustness across different models and defenses. To address this, we introduce TamperBench, the first unified framework to systematically evaluate the tamper resistance of LLMs. TamperBench (i) curates a repository of state-of-the-art weight-space fine-tuning attacks, latent-space representation attacks, and alignment-stage defenses; (ii) enables realistic adversarial evaluation through systematic hyperparameter sweeps per attack-model pair; and (iii) provides both safety and utility evaluations. We use TamperBench to evaluate 21 open-weight LLMs, including defense-augmented variants, across nine tampering threats using standardized safety and capability metrics with hyperparameter sweeps per model-attack pair. The results provide insights including effects of post-training on tamper resistance, that jailbreak-tuning is typically the most severe attack, and that current alignment-stage defenses largely fail to withstand attack sweeps. Code is available at https://github.com/criticalml-uw/TamperBench.
中文标题/摘要
标题：TamperBench：系统性压力测试LLM在微调和篡改下的安全性
随着越来越强大的开放权重大型语言模型（LLM）的部署，提高其对不安全修改（无论是意外还是故意）的抗篡改能力变得至关重要，以最小化风险。然而，目前没有标准的方法来评估抗篡改能力。不同的数据集、指标和篡改配置使得比较不同模型和防御的安全性、实用性和鲁棒性变得困难。为了解决这个问题，我们引入了TamperBench，这是第一个统一框架，用于系统性评估LLM的抗篡改能力。TamperBench (i) 收集了最先进的权重空间微调攻击、潜在空间表示攻击和对齐阶段防御的库；(ii) 通过对每个攻击-模型对进行系统的超参数搜索，实现现实的对抗性评估；(iii) 提供安全性和实用性评估。我们使用TamperBench评估了21个开放权重LLM，包括增强防御的变体，针对九种篡改威胁使用标准化的安全性和能力指标，并对每个模型-攻击对进行超参数搜索。结果提供了包括后训练对抗篡改能力的影响、越狱微调通常是最严重的攻击，以及当前对齐阶段防御在抵御攻击时大多失败的见解。代码可在https://github.com/criticalml-uw/TamperBench获取。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the critical need for evaluating the tamper resistance of large language models (LLMs) as their deployment increases, particularly against unsafe modifications. Previous methods lacked a standardized approach, leading to difficulties in comparing safety and robustness due to varied datasets and metrics. The proposed TamperBench framework differs by providing a unified system to systematically assess tamper resistance, incorporating a repository of attacks and defenses, and enabling comprehensive evaluations through hyperparameter sweeps. This paper contributes by offering insights into the vulnerabilities of LLMs, revealing that jailbreak-tuning poses significant risks and that existing defenses are often ineffective. The methodology involves evaluating 21 open-weight LLMs against nine tampering threats using standardized metrics, demonstrating the framework's capability to support robust safety assessments.

When Autoregressive Consistency Hurts Safety Alignment
Authors: Bochen Lyu, Yiyang Jia, Xiaohao Cai, Zhanxing Zhu
First: 2026-06-02T19:32:23+00:00 · Latest: 2026-06-02T19:32:23+00:00
Comments: 21 pages
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Safety alignment in large language models (LLMs) is fragile in part because it is often shallow: fine-tuning mainly reshapes the model's behavior near the first few output tokens. We argue that this phenomenon can be understood through autoregressive consistency, the tendency of next-token prediction to preserve and extend the current response trajectory consistently. By analyzing the learning dynamics of safety alignment, we show that autoregressive consistency can concentrate alignment updates on early tokens, offering a mechanistic explanation for shallow safety alignment. The same mechanism also predicts a broader class of attacks on LLMs: attacks that induce harmful continuation states at arbitrary positions in the output trajectory. As a concrete example, we introduce random insertion attack, which inserts a short harmful span into an otherwise safe refusal trajectory and exploits autoregressive consistency to sustain the resulting harmful branch, thereby bypassing safety alignment. Notably, a short harmful span can redirect the generation to be harmful even after a long refusal prefix, highlighting autoregressive consistency as a potential broader failure mechanism. This suggests that safety alignment should also break harmful autoregressive consistency throughout the output trajectory. We therefore propose adversarial safety alignment, an initial framework based on worst-case harmful continuation states, and instantiate it with random worst-insertion training. Overall, our results suggest that autoregressive consistency should be treated as a central consideration in both safety alignment and attack design.
中文标题/摘要
标题：自回归一致性如何损害安全对齐
大型语言模型（LLMs）的安全对齐脆弱，部分原因在于其通常较浅：微调主要重塑模型在前几个输出标记附近的行为。我们认为，这一现象可以通过自回归一致性来理解，即下一个标记预测保持并延续当前响应轨迹的一致性倾向。通过分析安全对齐的学习动态，我们表明自回归一致性可以将对齐更新集中在早期标记上，为浅层安全对齐提供了机制性解释。同样的机制也预测了一类更广泛的对LLMs的攻击：在输出轨迹的任意位置诱导有害延续状态的攻击。作为具体例子，我们引入随机插入攻击，该攻击将一个短的有害片段插入到一个原本安全的拒绝轨迹中，并利用自回归一致性来维持由此产生的有害分支，从而绕过安全对齐。值得注意的是，一个短的有害片段可以在长拒绝前缀后重新引导生成变得有害，突显自回归一致性作为潜在的更广泛失败机制。这表明安全对齐也应打破输出轨迹中有害的自回归一致性。因此，我们提出了对抗性安全对齐，这是一个基于最坏情况有害延续状态的初步框架，并通过随机最坏插入训练进行实例化。总体而言，我们的结果表明，自回归一致性应被视为安全对齐和攻击设计中的核心考虑。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the fragility of safety alignment in large language models (LLMs), which is often superficial due to fine-tuning that primarily influences the initial output tokens. Previous methods have focused on aligning model behavior without considering the implications of autoregressive consistency, which can lead to shallow safety alignment and vulnerability to attacks that exploit this consistency. This paper proposes a novel approach called adversarial safety alignment, which emphasizes breaking harmful autoregressive consistency throughout the output trajectory. The methodology involves analyzing learning dynamics and introducing random worst-insertion training to mitigate risks associated with harmful continuation states. The findings indicate that the proposed method effectively enhances safety alignment by addressing the limitations of existing techniques, demonstrating its potential to improve model robustness against specific attack vectors.
本研究探讨了大型语言模型（LLMs）中安全对齐的脆弱性，这种脆弱性往往仅限于初始输出标记，原因在于自回归一致性。以往的方法主要集中于微调，导致安全对齐浅显且易受攻击，这些攻击利用了这种一致性。所提出的方法，即对抗性安全对齐，旨在通过考虑整个输出轨迹中的有害延续状态来缓解这些问题，而不仅仅是关注开头部分。该方法包括随机最差插入训练，以增强对抗攻击（如随机插入攻击）的安全对齐能力，这种攻击即使在安全前缀之后也能重新引导有害输出。研究结果表明，解决自回归一致性问题对于改善安全对齐和设计强健的防御措施至关重要。
